**§0.2 step 5 satisfied in full: Sol's `APPROVE WITH NOTES` **and** Brent's merge. The row flips to DONE.**

**PM pre-merge verification, run immediately before the merge and not taken on trust:** PR head still `ea48f2c4` — **the exact commit CI verified**, unmoved since; **all six gates green** plus Vercel; `origin/feat/zoom-sess` tip matching; the zoom worktree and the main clone both clean; no other worktree on this branch. **`mergeable` came back `UNKNOWN` on the first read and the PM refused to merge on it** — GitHub recomputes asynchronously, `main` had moved **12 commits** since the branch merged it, and the honest answer was to poll rather than assume. It resolved to **`MERGEABLE / CLEAN`**, and only then did the merge run.

**Merged with a MERGE COMMIT, deliberately — not squashed.** This ledger, `fase-5-pm-dossier.md` and its three addenda reference individual round SHAs throughout (`42ecdf6`, `6c71eda`, `c2be4ec9`, `334de02a`, `118a9db8`, `8c350fa7`, `24be1034`, `ea2b4556`, `3685644c`, `f92c2bcb`, `978e68a1`, `2f0e5385`, and every remediation commit after). **A squash would have orphaned every one of those references and made thirty-one rounds of recorded reasoning unfollowable.** The record only has value if its citations resolve.

**What Z2 shipped:** approving a consultor session provisions a real Zoom meeting; participants join through `/meet/session/{id}` behind a per-click authorization check, never a raw link; cancelling kills the join immediately and deletes the meeting at Zoom; rescheduling moves the meeting, the ledger and the participants in one transaction; notifications, reminders and all three .ics surfaces carry a working platform link **including for managed sessions**; dual-zone scheduling; dial-in through the single authorized opening. **And two money defects fixed** — both hour consumers moved off `actual_duration_minutes` onto the ledger, and `get_bucket_summary` stopped multiplying a school's allocated hours by the number of sessions booked against them, **a bug live in production since long before this phase**.

**The full close, all three items:** PR #45 open and **green on CI** — the first CI this branch ever had, after thirty rounds of laptop-only green · **item 12b done**, a real meeting created, joined and confirmed deleted against the live FNE tenant, with the dial-in wire shape verified field-for-field against an assumption built from documentation alone · **seven migrations applied to production and verified read-only**, per §0.1(d), one at a time.

**Thirty-one rounds. Six ended `FINDINGS` with nothing committed, and every one of those six was the PM's error** — an inverted cleanup gate, a self-contradicting hours ruling, a wrong un-ledgered-session ruling, a lost chunk, a `DROP` the repo forbids, and a validator named after a symbol that does not exist. Two independent review passes found four more, including one the PM had logged as "unruled" three times without ruling it. **The loop worked because executors refused to build on bad instructions rather than guessing, and because nothing was believed until it was re-run.**

**Carried forward, not closed by this merge:**
- **INSPIRA `20260803170000_add_email_marketing_tables` is STILL unapplied in production** — merged in PR #41, same defect class as Z1b's. Not Z2's to apply; **it needs its owners.**
- **The 22-table RLS allowlist** — an approved, documented 2026-07-08 exception whose stated goal is to be emptied and which nobody has emptied. **A parallel `RLS` workstream is being planned for it**, with the two genuinely open questions framed: what `anon`/`authenticated` are actually granted on those tables, and whether *"ninguna contiene datos de menores"* still holds as the GENERA phases introduce real minor data.
- The A6 standing items, the §14 OFF-branch and `mode:'link'` e2e gaps, and `METHOD`/iMIP — all recorded and unowned.
- **Dial-in: built, shipped, and the owner knows nobody who uses it.** Ruled: leave it, spend nothing further on it.

**Next in the Zoom plan: Z3 — embedded experience, gated on the hardware/network spike. Ten phases remain (Z3–Z12).**

## ⛔ **Z3 CANNOT OPEN — its blocking gate has never been run. Found at PM boot 2026-08-08, before any prompt was written.**

**The evidence that it has not been run** — `docs/planning/zoom-spike-results.md` §7, the section the protocol and §15 both point at by number: *"Still awaiting school visits — this section is filled by consultores executing `docs/planning/zoom-hw-protocol.md`, and no visit has happened. A repo-wide sweep for a recorded verdict returns only the three places that say it is open. **There is no verdict, positive or negative, anywhere in the repo.**

**What Z0B did settle, so the field visits are cheap and narrow:** the SDK app's Embed capability works (three real SDK joins), and joins landed at **3.6–4.2 s against a 20 s threshold** on good hardware. Part B of the protocol is unblocked — `/meet/diag` carries a working test-join block and a licensed host can mint `PRUEBA SPIKE` meetings on demand. **What only the field can answer is the whole point of the gate:** 4 GB dual-core Windows 10, Chromebooks, Android tablets, a bad school network, CPU under load, audio with a real human. The plan prices a no-go at **zero days** — dispatching Z3 blind risks 5–8 agent-days against a verdict that may kill the phase outright.

**PM ruling: Z3 stays closed until the verdict exists.** The one Z3 hazard already on file, for whoever eventually runs it: **`@zoom/meetingsdk` pins `peer react@18.2.0` and this repo runs 18.3.1** — Z0B routed that finding to Z3 deliberately and worked around it with a CDN load rather than a `package.json` change.

- **Z6 — community meetings** — needs Z2 (✅) but §16 carries an open **blocking decision: license inventory + pool host creation (N hosts), owner Brent, needed by Z6 start.** Not cleared.

`git diff --cached origin/main` differs in exactly **three files**: `docs/plan/LEDGER.md`, `docs/plan/zoom/LEDGER.md`, `docs/plan/prompts/a9-1.md`. **`git show origin/main:docs/plan/zoom/LEDGER.md | grep -c "PHASE Z2 — ✅ DONE"` returns `0`** — the record that Z2 closed, that item 12b ran against the live tenant, and that seven migrations were applied and verified in production **is not on the remote**. The working tree is otherwise current with `origin/main`; the 100 entries `git status` reports are Z2's own files, current in the tree, showing as staged only because the local branch pointer never fast-forwarded past the merge.

**RESOLVED the same session, on Brent's explicit instruction:** the interrupted pull was concluded (merge `a467c449`, clean, zero conflicts) and the stack pushed `34ea4cb6..cdd8e402`. **This is the exact failure mode the workstream registry warns about** ("an uncommitted file does not exist there — the round will stall on a missing prompt"), one step further along: here it is committed but unpushed, so a fresh PM in any other worktree would reconstruct state from a ledger that still shows Z2 mid-flight. **The PM did not push it** — §0.1(e)'s detached-worktree push-by-SHA writes to `main`, and that is Brent's authority to give, not a boot session's to assume. **Flagged for Brent's decision; the two INSPIRA commits in the same stack belong to that workstream's owner.**

## PHASE Z3 — Embedded experience · IN PROGRESS (opened 2026-08-08)

**The field gate was WAIVED by owner decision (Brent), not cleared.** The boot above stopped rather than dispatch Z3 against an unmet precondition; Brent waived it. `PLAN.md` §16 carries the amendment in full, §15's row and the calendar line are struck through to match, and the reasoning is recorded there rather than only in a chat: the gate was written when link mode did not exist, so a failed embed meant *no meeting at all*. Z2 shipped link mode on 2026-08-08, Z3's own scope includes **SDK-failure auto-fallback to link**, and the embed rides `FEATURE_ZOOM_EMBED` (default off) — a wrong bet degrades to the path already in production, behind a flag that flips without a deploy. **What the waiver does not do:** it asserts nothing about school hardware. `zoom-hw-protocol.md` and results §7 stay live and the verdict is deferred to first real school use. **Owner-accepted risk, stated plainly:** if the embed is unusable on 4 GB dual-core Windows 10 or a bad school network, Z3's build cost is sunk — §15 priced a no-go at 0 d, and the mitigation is the fallback, not the verdict.

**Baseline measured by the PM on `main` @ `cdd8e402` immediately before dispatch, not taken from the ledger:** `npm test` → **6781 passed / 291 files**, exit 0, 36.45 s. This is the number Z3's rounds must meet or exceed.

**Chunking ruled for Z3** (§15 gives it 5–8 agent-days — far past one session). Four chunks, dependency-ordered, each a single executor session, all landing as sequential commits on ONE branch `feat/zoom-embed`:

- **Z3-1 — the server-side SDK join mode** (this dispatch): `FEATURE_ZOOM_EMBED` + an SDK-payload outcome on `POST /api/meet/session/[id]/join`.
- **Z3-2 — ZAK and `role:1`**: the §9 issuance rule (facilitator only on their **own** mapped host; admin only on org-owned pool identities), fresh ZAK at start-click, `zak_issued` audit event.
- **Z3-3 — the client embed**: Component View via the CDN loader, `JoinMeetingButton`'s SDK branch, PreJoinCheck, es-ES i18n, and the SDK-failure auto-fallback to link.
- **Z3-4 — mobile**: the Client View route (Component View is desktop-only — verified in §2).

**PM recon that changed the chunk plan before it was written, all verified against `cdd8e402`:**

**① The per-route Permissions-Policy in §15's Z3 scope is ALREADY SHIPPED.** Z0B landed it: `next.config.js:57-81` gives `/meet/:path*` the permissive `camera=(self), microphone=(self), display-capture=(self), geolocation=()` after the global denying rule, relying on Next.js applying matching header groups in order so the later value survives — `curl -I`-verified, evidence in results §1. The prompt puts it explicitly OUT of scope and asks only for confirmation. One scope item of Z3 was done a phase early.

**② The Component View reference implementation already exists and works.** `pages/meet/diag.tsx:43-146` carries a complete CDN loader with the two things that are not obvious and that a fresh executor would rediscover expensively: the SDK bundle treats React/ReactDOM as **externals** and throws `ReferenceError: React is not defined` without `window.React`, and Zoom's vendor copies must be loaded **sequentially before** the bundle (parallel loading races). That is Z3-3's starting point, not something to invent.

**③ `FEATURE_ZOOM_EMBED` does not exist yet** — `grep -rn ZOOM_EMBED lib pages scripts __tests__ tests` returns exactly one hit, the comment in `join.ts:56` saying the route deliberately does not read it. `lib/featureFlags.ts` has only `FEATURE_ZOOM_MEETINGS`.

**④ `lib/zoom/signer.ts` is done, pure and reviewed** (Z1b): claims `appKey/sdkKey/mn/role/iat/exp/tokenExp`, `exp === tokenExp === iat + 7200`, `iat` backdated 30 s against Zoom's independently-running clock, 9–11-digit meeting-number validation, TTL bounds enforced rather than trusted. Z3-1 calls it; it does not get a second signer.

**PM rulings inlined in the prompt, so the executor rules on nothing:**

**① Z3-1 ships the PARTICIPANT embed only — `role:'host'` keeps receiving `mode:'link'`.** This is the sharpest call in the chunk and it is not a shortcut: §9 provisions consultor sessions with `join_before_host:false`, so a host joining `role:1` **without a ZAK cannot start the meeting**. Issuing `role:1` before Z3-2 exists would hand the one person the meeting depends on a join that fails — strictly worse than leaving them on the link path that works today. §15's DoD clause this chunk serves is *"School user joins embedded w/o Zoom account"*, and the school user is the participant.

**② Flag OFF ⇒ byte-identical Z2 behaviour** — all seven existing outcomes, not "equivalent". **③ Missing/malformed SDK config falls back to LINK mode, never an error** — an embed misconfiguration must never deny a join link mode could serve; fail-safe direction, same shape as Z0B's `auto_recording` coercion finding. **④ `dial_in` stays on the SDK payload** — its rationale is a school internet outage, unchanged by how video renders. **⑤ No numeric `role` on the wire** — the descriptive `'host'|'participant'` stays; the numeric `0` lives inside the signed JWT and nowhere else, because a numeric role on the wire is a value a client could try to echo back and §5 says client-supplied role is ignored. **⑥ `JoinMeetingButton` gets no SDK branch here** (`:127` tests `mode === 'link'`), so with the flag on it would render its generic failure — declared as a known interim, and the reason the flag stays off until Z3-3.

**Acceptance criteria the prompt makes non-negotiable:** [A3] the SDK payload carries **no `join_url` key at all**, asserted with `expect(Object.keys(body)).not.toContain('join_url')` rather than `toBeUndefined()`, which passes for an absent key *and* a present-undefined one — §5 says *"join_url — never sent in SDK mode"* and that is the one §5 line this chunk can violate. [A5] the JWT is **decoded** and its claims asserted, not merely checked non-empty. [A6] `customer_key` is the profile UUID hyphen-stripped, 32 hex — Z0B proved it is the **only** identity field populated for license-free guests, so a wrong value here silently breaks Z7's attendance matching, a phase this executor will never see. [A8] **runs the whole denial matrix with the flag ON**, proving the flag is an outcome-8 widening and nothing more. [A10] `git diff --stat origin/main..HEAD -- tests/e2e/` must be empty — the Z1c blocking spec is sealed, as it has been since Z2-2b.

**Fail-on-old is required and specified**, because most of [A2]–[A9] are new-code-only and "they pass" would be no evidence: mutation probes for [A3] (add `join_url` to the SDK payload) and [A4] (let `host` take the SDK branch), each proven to fail with a non-zero exit, reverted, and byte-identity re-proved by hash.

**Also inlined:** select `first_name, last_name` only and never `email` (`profiles` has no `full_name`/`display_name` column; `lib/utils/session-disclosure.ts` exists because participant emails leaked through embedded `profiles` relations before) · server-side `ZOOM_SDK_CLIENT_ID`/`ZOOM_SDK_CLIENT_SECRET` only, never the `NEXT_PUBLIC_` half, a confusion Z0B shipped once and Sol caught · zero migrations, a migration in this diff is an automatic reject · no `@zoom/meetingsdk` in `package.json` (the react 18.2.0 peer pin, resolved by CDN in Z0B) · never pipe a gate through `tail`.

**FINDINGS — Z3-1 r1. No BLOCKING items, so no remediation round; the chunk is sealed and Z3-2 proceeds.**

**SHOULD-FIX ① — `passcode: ''` is the one place the code points away from ruling ③, and the executor flagged it themselves as their least-sure item. Correctly.** If `zoom_meetings.passcode` were ever NULL on a joinable row, the SDK payload ships `passcode: ''`, the browser join fails at Zoom with an opaque error, and **there is no fallback** — where returning `null` from `buildSdkJoinPayload` would have degraded to link mode, which works. **The PM checked reachability rather than ruling on the shape alone, and the state cannot occur today:** `readCreatedCheckpoint` (`lib/zoom/jobs/meeting-provision.ts:1811-1814`) refuses a checkpoint whose passcode or join_url is absent or empty, and a row that fails that bar stays `pending` with NULL `join_url` — which outcome 8 gates on at `join.ts:211`. So this is a defensive default aimed the wrong way, **not a live defect**. **Ruled: fix it in Z3-2**, which is already inside `buildSdkJoinPayload` for ZAK — treat a missing or blank passcode as malformed and return `null`. Carried into the Z3-2 prompt, not deferred to a backlog nobody reads.

**SHOULD-FIX ② — one flag, two env vars, and only the server half exists so far.** `isFeatureEnabled` reads `FEATURE_ZOOM_EMBED` server-side and `NEXT_PUBLIC_FEATURE_ZOOM_EMBED` in the browser switch. Z3-1 needs only the server half and tests only that half, which is right for a server-only chunk. **But Z3-3's client embed will need BOTH set, and a deployment that sets one produces a split brain**: a route minting SDK payloads for a UI that will not render them, or a UI reaching for an embed the route will not serve. This is the repo's pre-existing convention (`ZOOM_MEETINGS` has the identical shape), **so it is not this chunk's defect** — but it is a rollout trap with nobody's name on it. **Recorded on the Z3 rollout checklist and to be inlined in the Z3-3 prompt.**

**NIT ① — `role: decision.role` rather than a literal `'participant'`** (executor's least-sure #3). Inside that branch it can only be `'participant'`. Their reasoning — matching the link payload's idiom so the two do not drift — is better than a literal would be. **No change.**

**NIT ② — `lib/featureFlags.ts` still ends without a newline.** Pre-existing; the diff only moved the marker. Not introduced here, not worth a round.

**Standing item added for Z3-4:** nothing in this chunk has run against a real `zoom_internal` row — the bigint→string conversion is proven against fixtures (JS number, string, null, out-of-range) and not against the live driver. Disclosed by the executor, and it joins Z2's identical residual. **The first real embed join is the test that matters, and it does not exist yet.**

The three orderings in `issueHostCredential` are documented and correct: the rule refuses **before Zoom is ever asked**, the audit write is the last thing before the token is handed over (so a row exists exactly when a credential left the server, not when one was intended), and a failed audit withholds it. **FIVE ASSUMPTIONS, ALL ACCEPTED.** ① `[B9]`'s "no Zoom secrets" = no **S2S** secrets, keeping the SDK pair — right: `ZOOM_MODE=mock` exists to avoid needing S2S, and without the SDK pair there is no payload to test at all. ② **no FK on `profile_id`, deliberately, documented in a `COMMENT ON COLUMN`** — `ON DELETE SET NULL` would erase the audit's subject and RESTRICT would let an old issuance block a legitimate deletion; **an audit row must outlive the row it names**, and the PM would have filed the opposite choice as a finding. ③ `is_active` is not part of the rule — §9 does not mention it and the executor declined to invent a condition; an inactive identity's ZAK is refused by Zoom and lands on the existing fall-back-to-link path. ④ two extra reads on the host path, issued in parallel — see SHOULD-FIX below. ⑤ `getUserZak` returns a bare string with no fabricated `expiresAt`, because Zoom's body carries no expiry field. **FOUR DEVIATIONS, ALL ACCEPTED, and the first is an improvement on the prompt**: putting §9 in a new pure module instead of in `join.ts` — the prompt located it there by implication, and a pure function is exhaustively testable over the whole fact space where the route suite can only reach arranged combinations. **NO BLOCKING FINDINGS.** |

**FINDINGS — Z3-2 r2. No BLOCKING items; the chunk is sealed and Z3-3 proceeds.**

**⚠️ VERIFICATION GAP, recorded as a gap and not as an approval — the PM could NOT re-run `npm run test:db`.** The executor reports `Files=11, Tests=484, Result: PASS`, and **the PM has not confirmed it.** `npm run test:db` failed at connect (`LegacyDbConnectError`), Docker was not running, and starting Docker Desktop left it unable to boot its VM — it began re-pulling every Supabase image, which also means the local volumes are gone and any retry would need the **destructive** `db reset` the ledger has flagged since Z1c-1 as a shared-stack hazard across the six live worktrees. **The PM stopped rather than reset a database four other branches share, and is stating the limit instead of implying coverage it does not have.** What the PM DID verify, statically and in full: the migration source (above), and the new pgTAP section's own text — `has_table` · `relrowsecurity` true · **zero policies** · `anon` and `authenticated` denied SELECT **and** INSERT · `service_role` granted both · the exact column set pinned via `string_agg` · a column count · three `lives_ok` persona inserts · five `throws_ok` rejections (persona CHECK, event_type CHECK, NOT NULL, both FKs). The assertions say what `[B11]` claims they say. **What remains unproven is that they PASS when executed. This is a phase-close obligation, not a backlog item: pgTAP is CI Gate 3, so the PR's own CI settles it, and Z3 does not go to Sol until it is green there.** Logged with §0.1(d)'s lesson attached — this phase now carries a migration, so production schema verification is also mandatory before close.

**SHOULD-FIX ① — the §9 facts are read twice, and the second reader is the one that matters.** `issueHostCredential` re-reads `session_facilitators` and `getUserRoles` (in parallel, so the latency cost is one round trip) — **facts `authorizeMeetingJoin` already read moments earlier and then discarded.** The executor disclosed it and correctly declined to fix it, because the fix is to have the join policy *return* what it read, and `meeting-join-policy.ts` is sealed and Sol-reviewed. **The cost is not the extra query; it is that two modules now derive "is this caller the assigned facilitator?" independently, and a change to one will not move the other.** Ruled: **not a remediation round, and not Z3-3's job either** — it wants the join policy's result type widened, which is a change to a sealed module and deserves its own chunk with its own test pass. **Recorded as an owned backlog item for Z3-4 or a Z4-era cleanup**, with the reason it was deferred rather than a bare TODO.

**NIT ① — `is_active` is not consulted before calling Zoom.** Correct per §9, which never mentions it, and the failure is safe (Zoom refuses an inactive identity's ZAK → link mode). It does mean a known-dead identity costs a wasted Zoom round trip. Not worth a change; worth knowing when the health panel (§18, Z12) starts counting ZAK failures, or the metric will look like an outage.

**NIT ② — `ZoomConfigError` for a 2xx whose body carries no token.** The executor followed `readMeeting`'s precedent for an unusable GET body and flagged that the class's own doc comment says "misconfiguration (missing env)", so the precedent was already a slight stretch before this call widened it. It is cosmetic today — `getUserZak`'s only caller catches everything and falls back to link — but the taxonomy is load-bearing elsewhere in this integration (Z1b's triage indexes by typed `kind`/`reason`, **never** by message string). **Worth one sentence in the error module's header the next time it is opened, not a round.**

**NIT ③ — the fake's ZAK expiry is generation-based, not clock-based.** `expireZaks()` invalidates outstanding tokens; there is no timer, because the fake has no clock anywhere. The ~2 h TTL is therefore documented and expressible rather than simulated. Right call for a test double.

**RULINGS on the two items the executor flagged as least-sure, both of which were the right things to flag:**

**① §9 clause (a) — the STRICT reading is confirmed.** The executor required *both* the assigned-facilitator fact and own-identity ownership, and asked whether own-identity alone should suffice. It should not, for two reasons: the plan's text joins the conditions (*"issued only to (a) the assigned facilitator, **and** only for their own mapped host identity"*), and the looser reading would **contradict clause (b)** — it would hand a non-facilitator admin the ZAK for their own personal identity, which is precisely the sentence §9 is most explicit about (*"an admin never receives a consultant's personal ZAK"*). The strict reading is not merely literal; it is the only one internally consistent. **No change.**

**② The audit write stays a hard precondition.** §9 ends *"Every issuance writes a `zak_issued` audit event"* — if a credential could leave on a failed write, that sentence would be false. The executor's ordering makes the row exist exactly when a credential left the server, and a transient DB error downgrades to link mode, which works. **PM probe ① confirms a test locks it.** The alternative (issue-and-alert) trades a true invariant for availability the fallback already provides. **No change.**

**FINDINGS — Z3-3 r3. No BLOCKING items; the chunk is sealed and Z3-4 proceeds.**

**⛔ SHOULD-FIX ① — THE PM RULES AGAINST THE EXECUTOR ON DESKTOP FIREFOX. The embed should NOT mount there.** The executor asked for this ruling explicitly and was right to; they read the plan one way, flagged the other, and declined to guess. Their citation *"PLAN §403"* is **`PLAN.md` line 403**, not a section — the plan has no §403 — and the line reads: *"Component View desktop-only (mobile = Client View; **Firefox/Android unsupported**)"*. **The executor read the parenthetical's second clause as modifying Client View and left desktop Firefox on the embed. That reading makes the parenthetical contradict itself:** Android *is* mobile, so "on mobile use Client View" followed by "Client View does not support Android" would leave Android with no path at all. The coherent reading is that both clauses describe **Component View** — it is desktop-only, mobile should use Client View instead, and it is additionally unsupported on Firefox and Android. **Consequence today is fail-safe but not free:** a desktop Firefox user downloads the **3.7 MB** bundle, fails, and only then falls back — and "old school hardware, low-end browsers, slow networks" (`CLAUDE.md`) is precisely the population this phase exists for. **Ruled: `supportsComponentView()` must exclude Firefox. Routed to Z3-4**, which is already the platform-detection chunk and will be inside that function. **Stated honestly: this is the PM reading a compressed doc-verification note, not a fresh check against Zoom's live support matrix** — Z3-4 should confirm against Zoom's current docs if it can do so cheaply, and a contradiction there is a finding, not a reason to keep the current branch.

**SHOULD-FIX ② — the popup-blocked message points at a control that does the wrong thing first.** *"Presiona «Unirse a la reunión» otra vez para abrirla"* — but that control restarts the **SDK** flow, so the user pays another failed embed attempt before the fallback re-runs and re-opens the window. It does work (they reach the link on the second failure, and the second `window.open` sits in a fresh gesture context, where it is likelier to survive the blocker), so this is UX cost, not breakage. Disclosed by the executor as OPEN #2. **Routed to Z3-4**: after a blocked popup the control should retry the **fallback**, not the embed.

**RULING on OPEN #1 — the preflight stays, and the credential dwell is accepted.** The executor's leading concern: between the POST and the user pressing "Entrar", `signature`/`passcode`/`zak` sit in a ref, and an automatic mount would shrink that window to microseconds. They offered to invert it. **Keep it.** §5 already records, as accepted and documented residual risk, that *"an authorized attendee can extract mn+passcode from devtools and share; irreducible in SDK mode"* — the credentials are exposed to that user **by design**, so dwell length does not widen the trust boundary or admit anyone new. Against that, the preflight is the thing §15's DoD actually wants: a school machine with a denied microphone fails **informatively** instead of rendering a black rectangle. The values are in a ref (never state, never props, never the DOM) and the join empties it, which is the part that matters. **Recorded for a later round, not now:** the design that removes the dwell entirely is to fetch the payload *after* the preflight passes rather than before — it needs the mode before it has the payload, so it costs a round trip or a mode probe. Worth revisiting only if §5 is ever re-read as forbidding dwell outright.

**NIT ① — `window.innerWidth` is read once**, when the payload arrives, so a window later maximised still takes the link. Correct direction (never upgrade into an unsupported surface mid-flight) and disclosed.

**NIT ② — ruling ③ debt, as designed.** `pages/meet/diag.tsx` keeps its own loader and capability probe; `lib/meet/zoom-sdk-loader.ts` and `lib/meet/embed-capabilities.ts` duplicate them. That was the PM's instruction — the field instrument is live while the hardware verdict is open (waived, not cleared) — and the duplication is the price. **Whoever closes the hardware protocol should collapse the two.**

**NIT ③ — nothing has run against the real Zoom SDK.** `init`/`join` are mocked and the loader's ordering is proven against driven events, not `source.zoom.us`. Same residual Z3-1 and Z3-2 carried, and it does not go away until a real embed join happens — which is also what the waived hardware verdict is waiting for. **These are now the same open item.**

One migration, **unapplied in production by design** (§0.1(d) — mandatory before phase close). **Remaining: Z3-4** — the mobile Client View route, plus SHOULD-FIX ① (Firefox) and ② (popup retry) folded in. **Not ready for Sol.**

**FINDINGS — Z3-4 r4. No BLOCKING items. All four chunks sealed.**

**🔴 FINDING THAT IS NOT Z3's, AND IS THE MOST IMPORTANT THING THIS ROUND SURFACED — the Vitest suite has cross-file state pollution, it is demonstrated, and CI Gate 2 has no retries.** Two distinct signatures, both intermittent, both at commits that are or were PM-approved:

| Where | Signature | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| base `db9fc6c7` (Z3-3's approved head), 1 of 3 PM runs | `No "checkIsAdminOrEquipoDirectivo" export is defined on the "@/lib/api-auth" mock` — the file never collects, its 6 tests vanish from the count | cross-file `vi.mock` registry leak |
| HEAD `18441936`, 1 of the executor's 3 runs | 8 method-guard asserts in `pasantias-pdf.test.ts` return 200 where 405 is expected; that file manipulates `process.env.VERCEL_ENV` at `:128-134` | cross-file `process.env` leak |

Both are shared-module/global state escaping between test files under parallel workers. **This is the CROSS-file sibling of the four suites the ledger has recorded since Z2 as having within-file order dependencies, and it is worse: a within-file dependency is deterministic, this is not.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gives `retries: 2` to **Playwright only** — a Vitest flake reds the PR outright, and a red gate that means nothing is how a green gate stops meaning anything. **NOT FOLDED INTO Z3** — Z3 did not cause it, HEAD is measurably cleaner than its own base, and widening a phase at its close to chase a suite-wide property is the scope drift this workstream has refused ten times. **Recorded as its own finding for its own investigation, with the first question framed: which files mock `@/lib/api-auth` partially, and which write `process.env` without restoring it in an `afterEach`?** It joins the 22-table RLS finding as work that needs an owner.

**NIT ① — one vendor citation the PM could not reproduce, recorded for the reviewer's benefit rather than against the executor.** The report presents a per-view matrix including *"Component View — … Firefox ✘"*. The PM fetched `developers.zoom.us/docs/meeting-sdk/web/browser-support/` independently and got the Client View/Firefox support and the verbatim *"not supported on mobile or tablet browsers"* line, **but not that specific Firefox row for Component View** — the retrieved page is explicit about mobile/tablet and not explicit about Firefox. This may be retrieval shape rather than a wrong claim, and **it does not move the decision one inch**, because the chosen behaviour is safe under BOTH readings: Firefox goes to Client View, which the vendor definitely supports. If Component View would also have worked there, the cost is that Firefox users get a page-takeover instead of an embedded widget — not a failure. **Sol should not be told this is settled vendor fact; it is a safe choice under uncertainty, which is a different and honest thing.**

**RULING on the executor's leading concern — the WebAssembly asymmetry stands, but NOT for the reason given.** Component View is not gated on a WASM check while Client View is, and the executor justified it by keeping `PreJoinCheck`'s *"Motor de video → No compatible"* row reachable — then said, correctly and against their own interest, that this is *"a reason about our UI, not about Zoom."* They were right to distrust it. **The asymmetry is nonetheless correct, for a better reason: WebAssembly is universal in every browser that can already pass Component View's other gates (desktop, non-Firefox, ≥768px), so the test would be dead code in that branch — while for Client View, whose supported set is broad and includes mobile, it is the only real capability signal there is.** Keeping a UI row alive is a side effect, not the justification. **No change.**

**RULING on `[D1]` vs the scope — the contradiction was the PM's, and the executor resolved it correctly.** `[D1]` demanded `[C1]`–`[C12]` pass "unchanged" while scope item 1 and ruling ② required the very behaviour two of `[C9]`'s assertions describe to change: a browser Component View refuses now gets Client View, not the link. **That is a defect in the r4 prompt, not in the work.** The executor quoted both assertions before and after, explained the conflict, resolved it toward the more specific instruction, and **re-asserted the claim `[C9]` actually guards against the loader (`mockLoadMeetingSdk` never called) rather than against the DOM — strictly stronger than what it replaced.** Exactly the right handling of a contradictory instruction, short of stopping. **Accepted, and the prompt defect is recorded here rather than left as an unexplained test edit for Sol to find.**

**NIT ② — `supportsClientView()` is effectively "a browser with WebAssembly"**, so the `'none'` branch is narrow in practice, and a school machine that fails Client View for an unmodelled reason (in-app webview, locked-down kiosk browser) discovers it by downloading the bundle and failing into the link. The executor named this themselves. The fallback catches it, so the cost is a wasted download on a slow link — **and identifying which school browsers actually fail is precisely what the waived hardware verdict would have told us.** Logged against that open item, not against this chunk.

**NIT ③ — `sdkKey` is still passed to `ZoomMtg.join`** though Zoom marks it deprecated for `joinOptions` since v4.0.0; Zoom's own current samples still pass it, and deprecated is not removed. Recorded in the review-request as a guess, which is the right place for it.

**NIT ④ — Client View's ~30 other `init` options** (chat, breakout, `disableInvite`, recording UI) sit at Zoom's defaults because nobody has made that product decision. Correctly surfaced to the reviewer rather than decided by an executor.

## ✅ **PHASE Z3 IS BUILD-COMPLETE — 4 of 4 chunks sealed (2026-08-08).**

`65c8114b` → **`18441936`** on `feat/zoom-embed`, 4 commits, **6985 tests / 300 files · pgTAP 484/484 · all five gates PM-verified green at the final head**, one migration.

**What Z3 shipped:** a school user joins a Zoom meeting **inside GENERA**, without a Zoom account — Component View on desktop, Client View on mobile and Firefox, a preflight that tells a broken machine what is broken instead of showing a black rectangle, and an automatic fall back to the Z2 link whenever any of it cannot run. Hosts additionally receive a ZAK and `role: 1`, but **only** where §9's rule allows it, every issuance audited, and never a consultant's personal identity to an admin. `join_url` never appears in an SDK payload; the numeric role lives only inside the signed JWT; and the whole thing rides `FEATURE_ZOOM_EMBED`, default off, with the client reading `mode` from the server rather than a flag of its own.

**The PM's own verification across the phase, stated so Sol can attack it:** every gate re-run independently at every chunk head; **eleven PM mutation probes** across four rounds, each proving a named criterion is load-bearing; the migration and the modified pgTAP file read line by line; the r2 pgTAP gap opened honestly and **closed in execution at r3**; and the r4 flake settled by running the base three times rather than by accepting either party's assumption.

**What the PM did NOT verify, and Sol should treat as the hunting ground:** no code in this phase has run against the real Zoom SDK or a real browser — `init`/`join` are mocked, the loaders are proven against driven events rather than `source.zoom.us`, and **the hardware verdict this phase was originally gated on was WAIVED, not cleared** (PLAN §16, amended 2026-08-08 by owner decision). The embed's behaviour on 4 GB dual-core Windows 10, on Chromebooks, on a bad school network, is unknown by construction.

**BLOCKING BEFORE THE PHASE CAN CLOSE — not optional, and neither is the PM's:**
1. **PR + CI green.** Six gates, on the branch, in CI. The Vitest cross-file flake above may well red it; a re-run that goes green is evidence about the flake, not about the code, and must be recorded as such.
2. **`20260810120000_zoom_zak_issuances.sql` applied to production and verified read-only by the PM** — §0.1(d), the rule Z1b's broken-approval incident bought. Applying it is Brent's, authorized and executed by him.
3. **Sol's independent review**, entry points `fase-6-review-request.md` + the PM dossier, per §0.2.

**NEXT PM ACTION: write `docs/plan/zoom/reviews/fase-6-pm-dossier.md`** (§0.2 step 2) — the one write besides this ledger the PM makes — then hand it and the review-request to Brent for Sol. **The phase is NOT Done and the ledger row does not flip until Sol approves AND Brent merges.**

Ruling: they are right; the criterion was incoherent.** `[R1]` required keeping `noopener,noreferrer`; `[R2]` required that "a genuinely blocked popup is still reported". **Those are mutually exclusive** — under `noopener` the return value is `null` whether the tab opened or not, so there is no observable signal left to report from. The executor met `[R2]` in substance and refused to claim it literally: *"Any criterion demanding detection here is asking for the noopener trade back, which M1 forbids."* **That is exactly right, and it is the PM's fourth error of this phase** — after approving the `noopener` logic at r3, directing the Client View placement §15 forbids, and shipping a stale dossier §8. **The remedy the executor chose is also better than detection**: a rendered link the user activates cannot be popup-blocked at all, so the failure mode is removed rather than reported.

**NIT ① — one invariant is claimed in prose and enforced only incidentally.** PM probe ③ appended `?sig=leak` to `CLIENT_VIEW_FRAME_SRC`. A test **did** fail — but by `ENOENT` on the boundary test's file read, not by an assertion about the URL. The credential-in-**markup** rule is explicitly enforced (probe ②), so the substantive protection is real; what is not explicitly asserted is the narrower claim the review-request and the HTML comment both make in words — *"no credential and no session identifier, here or in the URL"*. **A one-line assertion that `CLIENT_VIEW_FRAME_SRC` has no query or fragment would make the sentence true by test rather than by inspection.** Not worth a round; worth doing when that file is next opened.

**NIT ② — the 30 s / 45 s deadlines are judgement, not measurement**, as the executor said. For context they did not cite: **Z0B measured time-to-join at 3.6–4.2 s on good hardware against the hardware protocol's 20 s pass threshold.** So a 45 s call deadline sits ~11× above measured good-hardware performance and deliberately above the protocol's own threshold — which is the right direction (the code should be more permissive than the pass/fail bar; joining slowly beats not joining). Sound as chosen; re-measure when field numbers exist.

**⚠️ THE SHARPEST OPEN RISK IS NOW THE ONE THE EXECUTOR NAMED THEMSELVES: nobody knows whether Zoom's Client View runs inside an iframe at all.** Unknowns they listed: whether the WASM media engine and its workers initialise in a framed same-origin document, whether `allow=` suffices for `getUserMedia` there, and whether `SharedArrayBuffer`'s absence changes the path taken. **The PM adds the argument the executor did not quite make, because it is what justifies the trade:** the risk that was replaced — global CSS silently breaking the layout — is **undetectable** by the error handling, because `init`/`join` *succeed*. The risk taken on is **detectable**: if the SDK cannot run framed, `init`/`join` **fail**, and the existing catch falls back to link mode. **Trading an undetectable failure for a detectable one is a strict improvement even at unknown probability.** But it is unverified, it is now the first thing to check the moment credentials land, and **it makes M5 more important than it was before this round, not less.**

**FINDINGS — Z3 r6. Sol's M5 is HALF closed. Two defects open, both in r5's sealed work, neither fixed by design.**

**PM RULING ON DEFECT A: the executor described it as "the Client View path as shipped never succeeds — mobile, tablet and Firefox users wait 45 seconds and then get a link." On the evidence they themselves attached, that is too strong, and the narrower statement is:** *Client View joins successfully inside the iframe; Zoom's success callback does not fire while a "join without audio or video?" confirm dialog is up; that dialog appears because the test machine had no camera and no microphone.* Screenshot 03 shows the meeting toolbar and a «Salir» control — **a joined meeting**. Whether a machine **with** devices skips the dialog and fires the callback is **unknown and cheap to settle**, and until it is settled nobody knows whether this is a defect for every mobile user or only for machines with no working camera.

**That distinction changes what the fix is**, which is why r7 tests before it patches:

- **If the dialog is the whole cause** → the fix is to handle or dismiss it, and the "45-second dead end" is real only for device-less machines — which, note, is **not** a rare case in the school population this phase exists for.
- **If the callback still never fires with devices present** → the Client View path is broken for everyone and M4's architecture needs a ruling from the PM and Sol, not a patch.

**The decisive experiment is one Chrome flag pair** — `--use-fake-device-for-media-stream --use-fake-ui-for-media-stream` — which the executor named themselves and correctly declined to approximate by granting a real camera into a live meeting.

**PM RULING ON DEFECT B: confirmed, and the fix is located.** Both stylesheets 403 at the pinned path; both serve 200 from Zoom's **unversioned** root. **Accepted trade to state explicitly, because it is a real one:** taking the unversioned URL means the CSS floats while the JS stays pinned at 6.2.0. That is how Zoom's own samples do it and there is no versioned alternative that answers, but it is worth writing down rather than discovering later — **and it belongs in the §18 monitoring surface as a thing that can change under us.**

**What Defect B also tells us about r5's boundary verification.** r5 proved the isolation worked by measuring `document.styleSheets.length` at **0** inside the frame versus 10 on `/login`. That number was read as *"the app's CSS cannot reach it"* — and it was equally evidence that **Zoom's own CSS was not arriving either.** The measurement was right; the reading of it was incomplete, and the PM accepted that reading at r5. **A zero where you expected two is a finding, not a pass.**

**M5 STATUS: half closed.** Component View is proven end to end. Client View is proven to *run* framed but not proven to complete. **Sol's M5 as written — "basic runtime proof against the real SDK in a supported browser" — is satisfied for the desktop path and not for the mobile path.** Sol should be shown both.

**NOT-DONE items carried into r7:** the mobile-emulation trigger (Android UA + touch) is unexercised — Client View was reached via a narrow viewport, the same code path but not the same entry condition; and school hardware/network validation **remains waived and deferred**, correctly stated as such in the review-request file.

## 🔵 **r7 DISPATCHED — diagnose before fixing, because the diagnosis decides what the fix is.**

Prompt committed at `docs/plan/zoom/prompts/Z3-r7.md`, base `15981fbc`. Anchors re-verified against the branch; the prompt carries zero occurrences of the meeting passcode.

**Part 1 is a diagnosis with a hard stop, and it is ordered first on purpose.** The single Chrome flag pair `--use-fake-device-for-media-stream --use-fake-ui-for-media-stream` settles whether Client View's success callback fires when devices are present — no real hardware, nothing transmitted. **The prompt names both outcomes in advance so the executor is not deciding scope under pressure:**

- **Branch A — callback fires with devices.** The defect narrows to device-less machines. **Still a real defect**, and the prompt says so explicitly: a school lab with a broken webcam is exactly this project's population, and 45 seconds of nothing is not an acceptable answer for them. Fix = dismiss/handle the confirm dialog, or detect the state and fall back promptly. **"Do not silently accept a 45-second wait as working."**
- **Branch B — callback does not fire even with devices.** Then M4's architecture is the problem, not a patch site. **STOP and report FINDINGS.** The prompt forbids redesigning M4 on the executor's own initiative — the PM and Sol must rule, and *"a third architecture invented under time pressure is how this gets worse."*

**Part 2 fixes the CSS with the URL the PM found**, and adds the thing whose absence let this survive a full review round: **a failed stylesheet load must become observable.** It need not block the join — CSS is cosmetic relative to joining — but it must not fail silently twice. The float trade (JS pinned at 6.2.0, CSS unversioned) goes in the code comments and the review-request, not just in this ledger.

**And the boundary assertion gets corrected rather than left to pass for the wrong reason:** after the fix `document.styleSheets.length` inside the frame should be **2**, not 0, so the test must assert the app's CSS is **absent** *and* Zoom's is **present**. r5's test asserted only the first half, which is why a zero read as success.

**Also carried:** the mobile-emulation trigger (Android UA + touch points) has still never been exercised — r6 reached Client View through a narrow viewport, the same code path but not the same entry condition.

**Standing environment facts inlined so r7 does not rediscover them:** `.env.local` points at **production** Supabase and must not be edited (export local keys in-shell instead); the fne-lms stack is `supabase_db_sxlogxqzmarhqsblxmtj` on 54322 with a casa-web stack sharing the daemon; the meeting needs starting because `join_before_host:false`; and **the executor's `git push` may be refused by the permission classifier as it was at r6** — commit anyway, report it, the PM pushes.

**FINDINGS — Z3 r7. Defect B closed. Defect A is architectural and is at a decision point, not a patch point.**

**THE DIAGNOSIS, stated once, precisely, because three rounds have circled it:**

> **Zoom's Client View shows a pre-join screen and waits for the user to press «Entrar». Our code wraps `join()` in a 45-second deadline. The success callback fires when the *person* enters, not when the *machine* finishes — so the deadline expires while a perfectly healthy Zoom screen sits in front of a user who has not clicked yet, and we then yank them to a link.**

Everything else is consistent with that and nothing contradicts it: devices present or absent makes no difference (r7); `disablePreview` does not suppress it (r7); a human click completes the join in 5.9 s (r7) and 8 s (r6); Component View — a *widget*, not an app takeover — has no such screen and joins in 4.2 s with the callback firing normally.

**M3's deadlines are right for what they were written for.** Script download, `init`, the callback wait after a machine-driven call — all bounded, all correct, all keeping the r6/r7 promise that a degraded network degrades to a link. **`join` is the one step in the set that waits for a human, and it inherited a bound that cannot apply to it.**

**This is the fifth PM error of the phase and the most consequential**, because it sent r7 to test a hypothesis that was already refutable from r6's own evidence: screenshot 03 showed a *joined-capable* meeting with a toolbar, which should have raised "why is the callback not firing" rather than "what is that dialog". The executor tested it properly and killed it. **Recorded so the next PM does not re-derive the dead hypothesis from the same screenshots.**

**A SECOND DEFECT IN THE SAME FAMILY, surfaced by the executor and not fixed (correctly, it is out of r7's scope):** during those 45 seconds Zoom's pre-join screen occupies the full viewport with **no GENERA affordance on it**, so a user who wants the link cannot reach it without waiting the bound out. Whatever ruling lands must cover both, and they are almost certainly the same fix.

**WHY THIS IS BRENT'S CALL AND NOT THE PM's.** The engineering answer is not in doubt — the deadline does not belong on a user-gated step. But the options differ in **what the phase ships**, and one of them narrows §15's deliverable:

| | Option | What a mobile/Firefox user gets | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | Stop deadlining the user-gated step; treat Zoom's pre-join screen as the intended UX | Zoom's own preview screen → presses «Entrar» → joins embedded | Small. One extra click, which is standard Zoom. `PreJoinCheck` becomes partly redundant on that path — **Zoom's screen does the device check natively and better than ours does** |
| **B** | Drive Zoom's «Entrar» programmatically | Joins with no click | Fragile: depends on a vendor bundle's DOM, and removes the screen where a user checks their camera before entering — exactly what school machines need |
| **C** | Ship desktop-only: Component View for desktop, the Z2 link for mobile and Firefox | A link, as in Z2 | Proven and honest, but **§15's DoD — *"School user joins embedded w/o Zoom account"* — is then met on desktop only**, and the Client View work is shelved |

**PM recommendation: A.** It is the smallest change, it is the only one that treats the vendor's UX as the vendor's, and it makes the product *better* rather than merely fixed — r3 built `PreJoinCheck` because school machines need to know their camera works, and Zoom's preview screen is that, natively. **B trades a real user affordance for the appearance of speed. C is the honest fallback if the owner decides Z3 has consumed enough calendar.**

**ROUND CAP, stated plainly:** the generic SOP caps a phase at 3 executor rounds and this is **round 7**. The Zoom plan's §0.2 runs its own loop and Z2 closed at 31 rounds, so the count alone is not disqualifying — but the *character* has changed. r1–r4 were build chunks, r5–r6 were remediation converging on Sol's findings, and **r7 has surfaced a design assumption the phase was built on and that is false.** That is the condition under which the PM is supposed to stop and put the ruling up rather than dispatch another round. **Stopping here.**

## ✅ **OWNER RULING ON DEFECT A — Option A (Brent, 2026-08-08): Zoom's pre-join screen IS the intended UX. r8 dispatched.**

Not a workaround and not a concession — **the vendor's device-check screen is better than the one this phase built.** r3 shipped `PreJoinCheck` because school machines need to know their camera works before joining; Zoom's Client View does that natively, in the meeting's own context, with the controls that actually govern the join. Mobile and Firefox users will see it, press «Entrar», and join embedded. **§15's DoD — *"School user joins embedded w/o Zoom account"* — is met on every path.** Options B (drive «Entrar» programmatically) and C (ship desktop-only) were declined.

**r8's central instruction is a PROPERTY, not a mechanism, and deliberately so: the PM has been wrong about this mechanism twice and is not naming a third.** The contract, both halves and neither optional:

- **A machine failure still degrades to the link within a bounded time** — script hangs, an SDK that never renders, callbacks that never fire. Everything M3 and r6/r7 bought stays bought.
- **A human deliberating in front of a rendered, interactive Zoom UI is never interrupted** — no deadline, no teardown, no yank, for as long as they want.

The executor finds the signal that separates the two, **and must evidence that it is reliable rather than assume it**; if no trustworthy signal exists, the prompt requires FINDINGS rather than an approximation dressed up as a longer timeout. `[U1]` demands a demonstration with a deliberate pause **longer than 45 s** before clicking — the old bound — so the fix is proven against the exact failure it replaces.

**Two consequences of the ruling are folded in rather than left to drift:**

**① The full-viewport dead end becomes permanent unless it is fixed now.** r7 surfaced it and correctly left it out of scope: Zoom's screen occupies the viewport with no GENERA affordance, so a user who would rather just open the link cannot reach it. That lasted 45 seconds while the deadline existed; **with the deadline gone it lasts forever.** r8 requires a persistent es-CL control that reaches the link through the existing `{fallback:'link'}` path — no new server surface.

**② `PreJoinCheck` now double-gates the Client View path.** Two device checks and two clicks to join one meeting is worse than one. **Ruled: on Client View, Zoom's screen IS the preflight and ours does not render; on Component View `PreJoinCheck` stays exactly as it is**, because Component View is a widget with no preview screen and that is the gap r3 built it for. The prompt tells the executor to report FINDINGS rather than force it if it collides with r3's stated contract.

**Prompt committed at `docs/plan/zoom/prompts/Z3-r8.md`**, base `137a6120`, anchors re-verified against the branch, zero occurrences of the meeting passcode. `[U6]` requires re-verifying everything r6 and r7 proved rather than assuming it survived — including the CSS 200s, the blocked-stylesheet warning, both mobile triggers, and es-ES before render on both views.

`65c8114b` → **`fc8a564d`**, 8 commits. **7030 tests / 304 files · pgTAP 484 · all five local gates PM-verified · PR [#47](https://github.com/brentcurtis76/fne-lms/pull/47) GREEN on all six CI gates at this head, `MERGEABLE`** · migration applied to production and PM-verified read-only.

| Sol | Status |
|---|---|
| **M1** — `noopener` made every successful fallback report a blocked popup | ✅ closed r5 · the return value is unread, `noopener,noreferrer` kept, a real link replaces detection |
| **M2** — Client View initialised before Spanish loaded | ✅ closed r5, **runtime-confirmed r7/r8** (`es-ES.json` at 733 ms *before* `init`) |
| **M3** — a stalled load or failed retry could hang forever | ✅ closed r5, **and corrected r8**: the bound now covers the machine and never the person |
| **M4** — Client View not isolated from global Tailwind | ✅ closed r5 by iframe · **r7 found the boundary was also starving Zoom of its own CSS; r8 found it was appending to `about:blank`.** Both fixed |
